IV. ANNOUNCEMENTS 
Supervisor Aguiar: “Good afternoon. Today is Tuesday, January 5, 202 1 
and we are here for a Town Board meeting. Happy New Year everyone. We 
look forward to a prosperous 2021 and are confident that this spring we will 
start recovering from the Covid 19 pandemic as long as the vaccine is being 
administered and people start g oing outdoors and we start seeing our Covid 
contamination rate decrease. As always we will begin with the pledge followed 
by an Invocation by Reverend Dr. Sean Murray from the First Congregational 
Church of Riverhead.” 
 
ANNOUNCEMENTS 
 
 Supervisor Aguiar: “Before we get started we have five brief announcements 
today. Covid 19 positive cases are continuing to rise at a very alarming rate in 
  
For more information visit our website at www.townofriverheadny.gov  
 
our area as we wait for the widespread distribution of the Covid 19 vaccine. 
Please insure that you follow the three “W’ s” that we use here in Riverhead. 
The three “W’s” are wear your mask, wash your hands and watch your distance. 
Get tested at a local facility. There are nine here in Riverhead and all the 
facilities, dates, times and locations are listed on our website in our Covid 19 
special folder that we have since the inception of Covid 19 contamination here. 
Second, the Town of Riverhead shotgun hunting for deer program at EPCAL 
and 1751 Sound Avenue, Calverton commence on January 3, 2021 and will 
terminate on January 31, 2021 inclusive by Town of Riverhead permit only. 
The recreational path, woods and the fields located there will also be closed to 
the public in the interest of safety, however the dog park will remain open. 
Please also note that the town owned property  located on Middle Road, 
Riverhead on the northside west of Mill Road is also closed until January 31 st 
inclusive due to bow hunting activities. The Riverhead Safe Program 
established at the beginning of Covid remains in effect. This is for our seniors 
and it’s available for anyone who needs assistance whether its purchasing 
essentials or food supplies. We also have an assistance with seniors. We 
recently started a senior, a Riverhead Senior Hotline. The number is 631 727 -
3200 Ext. 653. The hotline is avail able Tuesday and Thursday between the 
hours of 9:30 and 12:30; however, any phone calls, messages left on the hotline 
will be responded to within one day. We have also signed a memorandum of 
agreement with the Suffolk County for emergency delivery of food. We do have 
a substantial supply at the Riverhead Senior Center. It consists of, anyone can 
apply for this delivery; you call 311, you register and you will receive an 
emergency supply of food for four people, three days, three meals a day and if 
there’s m ore than four people, you’ll get two boxes and that’s delivered by 
either the police department, the senior center or my office so we are the ones 
making that delivery. Again, it’s available to anyone who may need it. Lastly as 
most of you know by now, whi le we remain under the state of emergency due 
to Covid 19, all public participation of our meetings and public hearings will be 
accomplished via zoom only. Accordingly, the public may watch or participate 
in any of today’s meeting events by, in three metho ds. One, viewing it live on 
television on Channel 22, second logging onto the town website in Riverhead or 
three using zoom with the meeting credentials that are posted on our website. 
Okay, moving forward today we are going to deviate slightly from the no rmal 
agenda sequence because we have so many special presentations to make today 
regarding the first few resolutions. So, first I’m going to ask Diane, our Town 
Clerk to review any correspondence, Diane.”

John McAuliff : “So Happy New Year to everyone. In a sense I’m 
following up directly with the letter I sent to you which has to do with both the 
earlier letter and  the letter last night on the question of the selection of a new 
member of the Board. It is a new year and a time always of new beginnings and 
I would hope that one of those new beginnings of the Board is to step back a 
moment and reconsider the procedure on choosing somebody to fill the 
vacancy. The, I looked back at the November 4 th meeting, actually watched the 
video which is of course more explicit than the minutes and in that, the 
Supervisor when I asked about how the vacancy was going to be filled, you said 
  
For more information visit our website at www.townofriverheadny.gov  
 
“we as a Board will meet and we will vote on an individual. We will vote on the 
replacement. Names will be put forward. People will send in their desire. 
Interviews will take place and we will vote on an individual”. Now it’s not clear 
that any of t hat has happened. Instead, although if you look at whereas clauses, 
the two resolutions, the first one for Marilyn Winter said that the candidates 
were screened and interviewed for the vacant position. I don’t know when the 
interviewing took place. It cert ainly did not take place in public for the benefit 
of the town population. The second where, in the second resolution the whereas 
said the Town Board has received and considered the applications of 
individuals. I think the problem is that essentially the B oard seems to have 
subcontracted its responsibilities. Instead of it interviewing people, receiving 
resumes and interviewing people, it was a Republican Town Committee that 
received eighteen, did eighteen interviews and it vetted people and then it 
proposed someone. I am in no sense speaking negatively about the person in the 
second resolution. I would note however there is absolutely nothing said about 
him while in the first resolution for Ms. Winter, there is quite substantive detail 
as well as a letter s he wrote to the Riverhead Local. So, I think you need to 
really think about what, this is not simply a political handover, but it is a serious 
choice that you need to make in open and in public giving people a fair shot at 
presenting their credentials and their intentions about what they would do. I 
want to end up by coming back to my original letter that I sent a month ago 
which is similar to the concern I expressed when the judicial appointment was 
being made. Riverhead is a town with a twenty percent Afr ican American 
population. Because of the at large nature of election to the Board, which might 
be looked at, but the at large nature of selection of elections, it is unlikely, it is 
not a surprise that there has been one African American member of the Board in 
all of its history and so the question is whether you use this opportunity to try to 
bring onto the Board somebody who reflects a different kind of viewpoint and 
experience and I’d note there is, we all several months ago were struggling 
what with the  issues of systemic racism and what that means. Systemic racism 
is not somebody thinking they’re a racist then consciously being prejudice, it’s 
what exists in the systems and if the system of an at large election means that a 
significant portion of the po pulation is not represented in the governance of the 
town, I think that’s a problem. So I ask you to consider Ms. Winter. I mean just 
looking at the documents, she’s obviously the better qualified because there’s 
no qualification for the other persons subm itted. I also note in her bio that she 
sent to (inaudible).” 
 
Devon Higgins : “Mr. McAuliff, thirty seconds. Mr. McAuliff, thirty 
seconds left.”

27. 2021-31  Maintains Policy of Non-Discrimination  
Councilman Hubbard:    “Maintains Policy of Non -
Discrimination, so  moved.” 
 
Councilwoman Kent:   “Seconded.” 
 
Supervisor Aguiar:   “Vote please.”   
 
 The Vote: “Beyrodt, yes;    Kent, yes;  Hubbard, yes;  Aguiar, yes. 
Resolution adopted.” 
 
 Supervisor Aguiar: “I’m sorry Catherine. Just let me go back to this 
Resolution 31. I just want to make note that during the time when we first 
closed down due to Covid in March, the town was supposed to 
commence on a nondiscrimination regulatory training session for all 
employees and there was a cost involved. We had to hire a firm and an 
attorney was involved and at my direction with the Personnel Director, 
they were able to ascertain training given by the state free of charge and 
people were able to do it in the comfort of their home. So, that’s an 
expense that we were able to save on and it was also state compliant 
because the state the state created it and it was at no cost.” 
RESULT: ADOPTED [UNANIMOUS] 
MOVER: Tim Hubbard, Councilman 
SECONDER: Catherine Kent, Councilwoman 
AYES: Yvette Aguiar, Tim Hubbard, Catherine Kent, Frank Beyrodt Jr.

TOWN OF RIVERHEAD NOTICE OF CHANGE IN TOWN BOARD MEETING 
PLEASE TAKE NOTICE that the Town Board Meeting scheduled for January 5, 2021 at 2:00 
p.m. at Riverhead Town Hall, 200 Howell Avenue, Riverhead, New York will proceed as scheduled.   
PLEASE TAKE FURTHER NOTICE, that in accordance with the directive of 
Executive Order 202.1 issued by Governor Andrew M. Cuomo, as extended by subsequent 
Executive Orders, the aforementioned meeting will be held without in-person attendance by the 
public. Instead the public can watch the January 5, 2021 meeting of the Town Board via the 
following methods:  
1. Live stream; namely, by viewing it on your television at Channel 22;  
2. Logging on the Town of Riverhead website, www.townofriverheadny.gov and clicking 
on the link “Channel 22” on Quick Links on the home page or by connecting to the URL, which 
is as follows: townofriverheadny.viebit.com/player_live.php  
3. Zoom Meeting, log in as follows:   
https://us02web.zoom.us/j/88486727130?pwd=WDlTdXNGM0dFYkM1N01EdzRQK0Zidz09 
Webinar ID:  884 8672 7130   Passcode: 000553 
Dial by your location: +1 929 205 6099 (New York)  
PLEASE TAKE FURTHER NOTICE, that the public will have an opportunity to via  
Zoom to be heard, to make their comments and/or raise questions regarding the public hearing 
and resolutions, before the Town Board of Town of Riverhead at the January 5, 2021 meeting.  
PLEASE TAKE FURTHER NOTICE, that the public will also have an opportunity via 
Zoom to be heard, to make public comment regarding any matter of concern relative to the Town 
Board after the Town Board has concluded with resolutions and the public hearing at the meeting 
held on January 5, 2021 
PLEASE TAKE FURTHER NOTICE, that upon entering the Zoom meeting, you will 
be placed in a virtual audience. From there, you may either passively watch the meeting live, or 
communicate with the moderator/host your request to make a comment during the public 
comment section for PUBLIC HEARINGS, RESOLUTIONS and/or OPEN COMMENTS. To 
get the attention of the moderator/host, use the “CHAT” or “RAISE HAND” feature, then use 
the “CHAT” feature to inform the moderator/host of the following: 
 
1) Name; 2) Hamlet; 3) Public Hearing, Resolution and/or Open Comment topic. 
 
If you are calling in, please dial *9 to “raise your hand”, so we can acknowledge your request to 
speak. 
 
When it is your turn to speak, your status will be temporarily changed so you can be unmuted 
and visible for the five-minute comment period. After five minutes, you will be placed back in 
the audience. 
 
All comments/questions will be limited to five minutes. Please be patient; your admittance will 
be based on the volume of participants. Any problems or issues getting to the Town Board 
meeting via telephone or computer should be directed to (631) 727-3200 x655.   
2.1
Packet Pg. 60
Communication: Notice  (NOTICE TO CHANGE TOWN BOARD MEETING)
 
01.05.2021 ADOPTED 
 
 
TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
 
Resolution 2021-1 
 
RESOLUTION ACCEPTING THE RESIGNATION OF TOWN COUNCILWOMAN JODI 
GIGLIO  AND ACKNOWLEDGING HER SERVICE TO THE TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
 
Councilman Beyrodt Jr. offered the following resolution, 
which was seconded by Councilman Hubbard 
 
 WHEREAS, Councilwoman Giglio was elected to the Town Board of the Town of 
Riverhead in November of 2009, and re-elected in 2013 and 2017 and is serving her 
third four year term which began in 2018; and  
 WHEREAS, Councilwoman Giglio has been a staunch advocate for protection of 
the environment, preservation of our agricultural community and heritage, code reform 
and enforcement to protect residents and businesses alike, and dedicated her tenure to 
the protection of the quality of life for all residents; and  
 WHEREAS, Councilwoman Giglio has actively participated in the reform of town, 
county and state laws for the betterment of the health, safety and welfare of all residents 
and businesses within the Town, County and State; and   
 WHEREAS, Councilwoman Giglio worked throughout the COVID pandemic to 
donate resources to hospitals, distribute food and essentials to those in need during the 
pandemic, and advocate for businesses that are suffering post-pandemic throughout the 
pandemic; and   
 WHEREAS, Councilwoman Giglio has tendered her resignation as a 
Councilwoman serving on the Town Board for the Town of Riverhead effective 
December 31, 2020.  
 NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED that the Town Board hereby accepts the 
resignation of Jodi Giglio from her service as Councilwoman on the Town of Riverhead 
Town Board effective December 31, 2020 and hereby offers its gratitude and 
appreciation for her service; be it further   
 RESOLVED, that all Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy of 
this resolution from the electronic storage device and, if needed, a certified copy of 
same may be obtained from the Office of the Town Clerk.  
 
THE VOTE 
 
RESULT: ADOPTED [UNANIMOUS] 
MOVER: Tim Hubbard, Councilman 
SECONDER: Frank Beyrodt Jr., Councilman 
AYES: Yvette Aguiar, Tim Hubbard, Catherine Kent, Frank Beyrodt Jr.

Packet Pg. 62
 
01.05.2021 SCHEDULED 
 
 
TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
 
Resolution 2021-2 
 
APPOINTS MEMBER TO THE TOWN OF RIVERHEAD TOWN BOARD 
 
Councilwoman Kent offered the following resolution, 
which was seconded by  
 
      WHEREAS, as a result of the 2020 Election of Councilwoman J odi A. Giglio to 
the New York State Assembly, the position of Town Council of the Riverhead 
Town Board has become vacant; and  
 
WHEREAS, candidates were screened and interviewed for the vacant position 
and upon careful consideration, the Town Board of the Town of Riverhead wishes 
to appoint Marilyn  Banks -Winter to fill the remaining term of Jodi A. Giglio as 
Town Councilwoman effective January 5, 2021; and 
 
WHEREAS, Marilyn Banks -Winter is a long -time resident (56 Years) of 
Riverhead, a local business owner, a U.S. Army veteran, and an elected Trustee of 
the Riverhead Free Library who has a deep understanding of our community.  
She has served our community in various capacities including as a member of 
the Town’s Downtown Revitalization Committee, the River head Chamber of 
Commerce, the Riverhead Central School District’s Diversity and Cross Cultural 
Task Force, the Hauppauge Industrial Assoc. (HIA), Founding President and Co -
Chair of the AAECF, Inc., and board member of East End Arts.  Ms. Winter has 
served in many leadership roles and is also a member of several other non-profits 
and advocacy groups whom have successfully brought legislation to local and 
state government entities.  Ms. Winter is also responsible for the opening of the 
Riverhead Satellite VA Clinic at the Riverhead County Center that provides 
medical and mental health services to veterans.   
 
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED that the Town Board hereby accepts the 
appointment of Marilyn Banks -Winter who is qualified to take office and serve in 
the capacity of Town Councilwoman, as if elected, at a municipal election for 
office for a term to expire on December 31, 2021; and be it further  
 
RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk is hereby directed to forward a copy of this 
resolution to Ms. Marilyn Banks -Winter (include a mailing address and an e -mail 
address per TC’s request); and be it further 
 
      RESOLVED, that all Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy of 
this resolution from the electronic storage device and if needed, a certified copy 
of same may be obtained from the Office of the Town Clerk.  
 
THE VOTE 
 
7.2
Packet Pg. 63
RESULT: NO ACTION TAKEN

Packet Pg. 65
 
01.05.2021 ADOPTED 
 
 
TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
 
Resolution 2021-3 
 
APPOINTS COUNCILMAN TO THE RIVERHEAD TOWN BOARD 
 
Councilman Hubbard offered the following resolution, 
which was seconded by Councilman Beyrodt Jr. 
 
WHEREAS, pursuant to letter of Councilwoman Giglio dated December 30, 2020 
Councilwoman Giglio resigned from her position as councilwoman of the Town Board 
effective December 31, 2020 leaving a vacancy; and  
 
WHEREAS, pursuant to Town Law §64 (5), the Town Board of the Town of 
Riverhead, by majority vote thereof, has the authority to appoint a qualified person to fill 
the vacancy until December 31, 2021; and   
 
WHEREAS, the Town Board has received and considered the qualifications of 
individuals that have expressed interest in filling said vacancy; and   
 
WHEREAS, pursuant to Town Law §64 (5), the vacant seat will be subject to an 
election at the general election in 2021; and   
 
WHEREAS, the Town Board seeks to appoint Kenneth T. Rothwell to fill the term 
remaining of Councilwoman Giglio; and  
 
NOWTHEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED, that the Town Board hereby appoints 
Kenneth T. Rothwell as Councilman to the Riverhead Town Board effective January 6, 
2021; and be it further   
 
RESOLVED, that all Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy of 
this resolution from the electronic storage device, and if needed, a certified copy of 
same may be obtained from the Office of the Town Clerk.  
 
THE VOTE 
 
RESULT: ADOPTED [3 TO 0] 
MOVER: Tim Hubbard, Councilman 
SECONDER: Frank Beyrodt Jr., Councilman 
AYES: Yvette Aguiar, Tim Hubbard, Frank Beyrodt Jr. 
ABSTAIN: Catherine Kent

01.05.2021 ADOPTED 
 
 
TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
 
Resolution 2021-4 
 
APPOINTS POLICE OFFICERS TO THE POLICE DEPARTMENT 
 
Councilman Beyrodt Jr. offered the following resolution, 
which was seconded by Councilman Hubbard 
 
 WHEREAS, the Suffolk County Department of Civil Service established List #19-
5002-027 Police Officer OC on January 10, 2020; and 
 
 WHEREAS, extensive background investigations and personal interviews were 
conducted by the Suffolk County and Riverhead Town Police Departments to establish 
ten (10) individuals eligible for hire by the Town of Riverhead Police Department. 
 
 NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, effective January 6, 2021, Matthew 
Staker, Logan Frati, Katerina Hatzinikolaou and Joseph Trivelli are hereby appointed to 
the position of probationary Police Officer for the Town of Riverhead Police Department; 
and 
 
 BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, effective January 6, 2021, Brett Drost is hereby 
appointed to the position of a Probationary Police Officer; and is immediately placed on 
unpaid leave of absence until his return from active military duty. On that day, the 
aforementioned individual will become an active probationary Police Officer for the 
 Town of Riverhead Police Department as long as he meets the requirements for 
employment and; 
 
 BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED effective January 6, 2021, Anthony Chiaramonte,   
Nelson Perez, Kevin Thomas, Cole Montefusco and Travis Wooten are hereby 
appointed to the position of probationary Police Officer and are immediately placed on 
an unpaid leave of absence until the commencement of the next scheduled recruit 
police officer training session at the Suffolk County Police Academy or an equivalent 
training academy.  On that day, the above-named individuals will become active 
probationary Police Officers for the Town of Riverhead Police Department as long as 
they meet the requirements for employment and; 
 
 BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that these appointments are contingent upon the 
candidates successfully passing a drug test administered by the Town of Riverhead; 
and 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk is hereby 
directed to forward a copy of this resolution to Matthew Staker, Logan Frati, Katerina 
Hatzinikolaou, Joseph Trivelli, Brett Drost, Anthony Chiaramonte, Nelson Perez, Kevin 
Thomas, Cole Montefusco, Travis Wooten, the Chief of Police, the Personnel Officer 
and the Financial Administrator.  Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy 
of this resolution from the electronic storage device, and if  needed, a certified copy of 
same can be obtained from the  office of the Town Clerk.

RESULT: ADOPTED [UNANIMOUS] 
MOVER: Frank Beyrodt Jr., Councilman

01.05.2021 ADOPTED 
 
 
TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
 
Resolution 2021-13 
 
AUTHORIZES STIPEND WITH TOWN ENGINEER 
 
Councilman Beyrodt Jr. offered the following resolution, 
which was seconded by Councilman Hubbard 
 
WHEREAS, the retirement of the Sanitation Supervisor in 2014 necessitated the 
reallocation of job duties among existing staff members,  making it incumbent upon the 
Town Board to assign the oversight and implementation of the Household Hazardous 
Waste Program to an appropriate individual; and 
 
WHEREAS, as per Resolution #19 of 2015, the Town Board appointed Town 
Engineer Drew Dillingham to perform the duties associated with this program; and 
 
WHEREAS, Drew Dillingham has agreed to continue these duties and 
responsibilities, and the Town Board likewise authorizes the continuance of a stipend in 
the amount of $3,000.00 to be paid for the year 2021. 
 
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED, that this Town Board hereby assigns the 
oversight and implementation of the Household Hazardous Waste Program to Town 
Engineer Drew Dillingham, and authorizes a stipend to be paid in the amount of 
$3,000.00 with no other change to the terms and conditions of his employment; and be 
it further 
 
RESOLVED, that all Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy of 
this resolution from the electronic storage device and, if needed, a certified copy of 
same may be obtained from the Office of the Town Clerk.   
 
THE VOTE 
 
RESULT: ADOPTED [UNANIMOUS] 
MOVER: Frank Beyrodt Jr., Councilman 
SECONDER: Tim Hubbard, Councilman 
AYES: Yvette Aguiar, Tim Hubbard, Catherine Kent, Frank Beyrodt Jr.

01.05.2021 ADOPTED 
 
 
TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
 
Resolution 2021-14 
 
RATIFIES ACCEPTANCE OF HIGHWAY SUPERINTENDENT’S PERSONNEL 
REPORT & REQUEST FOR TOWN BOARD RESOLUTION TO EFFECTUATE 
PERSONNEL CHANGE 
 
Councilman Hubbard offered the following resolution, 
which was seconded by Councilwoman Kent 
 
 WHEREAS, pursuant to Town Law § 64(1), the Town Board has general 
management and control of the finances of the Town; and 
 WHEREAS, pursuant to Town Law § 64(3), the Town Board has general 
management and control of Town property; and  
WHEREAS, pursuant to Town Law § 20 and except as otherwise provided by 
law, the Town Board has the responsibility to employ such persons as the Town Board 
deems necessary for the proper conduct of the affairs of the Town; and 
WHEREAS, pursuant to Town Law § 27, the Town Board shall fix, from time to 
time, the salaries of all officers, officials and employees of said town, whether elected or 
appointed, and determine when the same shall be payable; and   
WHEREAS, pursuant to Highway Law § 140(4), the Highway Superintendent 
shall employ such persons as may be necessary for the maintenance and repair of 
Town highways and removal of obstructions caused by snow and provide for the 
supervision over such employees, subject to the provisions of law recited above and, 
including but not limited to, available appropriations for such purpose, any applicable 
civil service requirements; and  
WHEREAS, the Highway Superintendent filed a Personnel Report and Request 
for Town Board Resolution to Effectuate Personnel Request, to wit: ratify the 
appointment of Kevin Purick to the position of Automotive Equipment Operator effective 
December 16, 2020; and 
WHEREAS, the Highway Superintendent’s report stated that said appointment is 
necessary for the repair and maintenance of Town highways; and 
WHEREAS, this position was duly posted in accordance with the terms of the 
CSEA contract, Job Posting #26.  
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that the Town Board accepts the 
Highway Superintendent’s Personnel Report and Request Town Board Resolution to 
Effectuate Personnel Request, to wit:  to ratify the appointment of Kevin Purick effective 
December 16, 2020 to the position of Automotive Equipment Operator and sets his 
salary at Group 6, Step P of the CSEA Operational and Technical Salary Schedule 
which is $46,687.54 per annum; and be it further 
15.10
Packet Pg. 104
RESOLVED, that the Personnel Officer and Financial Administrator are hereby 
directed to take such action to effectuate this personnel matter on behalf of the Highway 
Superintendent; and be it further      
 RESOLVED that the Town Clerk is hereby directed to forward a copy of this 
resolution to the Town Highway Superintendent, Personnel Officer, Financial 
Administrator and Town Attorney’s Office; and be it further   
RESOLVED, that all Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy of 
this resolution from electronic storage device and, if needed, a certified copy of same 
may be obtained from the Office of the Town Clerk. 
 
THE VOTE 
 
RESULT: ADOPTED [UNANIMOUS] 
MOVER: Tim Hubbard, Councilman 
SECONDER: Catherine Kent, Councilwoman 
AYES: Yvette Aguiar, Tim Hubbard, Catherine Kent, Frank Beyrodt Jr.

TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
 
Resolution 2021-15 
 
APPOINTS A PART TIME POLICE OFFICER 
 
Councilwoman Kent offered the following resolution, 
which was seconded by Councilman Beyrodt Jr. 
 
 WHEREAS, the need exists for Part-time Police Officers in the Riverhead Police 
Department; and 
 
 WHEREAS, Chief David Hegermiller has received an application from P.O. 
Sabina Costantini for said position; and 
 
 WHEREAS, P.O. Costantini did successfully complete her required training at 
the Suffolk County Police Academy class 19-179. 
 
 NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED, that the Town Board hereby appoints 
P.O. Costantini to the position of Part-time Police Officer, effective January 5, 2021, at 
an hourly rate of pay of $21.00; and 
   
RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk is hereby directed to forward a copy of this 
resolution to Sabina Costantini, the Chief of Police, the Personnel Officer and the 
Financial Administrator.  Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy of this 
resolution from the electronic storage device, and if needed, a certified copy of  same 
can be obtained from the office of the Town Clerk. 
 
THE VOTE 
 
RESULT: ADOPTED [UNANIMOUS] 
MOVER: Catherine Kent, Councilwoman 
SECONDER: Frank Beyrodt Jr., Councilman 
AYES: Yvette Aguiar, Tim Hubbard, Catherine Kent, Frank Beyrodt Jr.

Resolution 2021-22 
 
APPOINTS MEMBER TO THE  TOWN OF RIVERHEAD RECREATION ADVISORY 
COMMITTEE 
 
Councilman Hubbard offered the following resolution, 
which was seconded by Councilwoman Kent 
 
 WHEREAS, by Resolution # 2020-462 adopted on September 1, 2020, the 
Town Board approved an increase in the membership of the Town of Riverhead 
Recreation Advisory Committee from seven (7) members to nine (9) members; 
and  
  
WHEREAS, by Resolution # 2020-462 adopted on September 15, 2020, the 
Town Board did appoint an eighth (8th) member to Town of Riverhead Recreation 
Advisory Committee; and  
 
WHEREAS, by letter dated December 15, 2020, Brian D. Mills, in his 
capacity as the Chair for the Town of Riverhead Recreation Advisory Committee 
did recommend that Jeremy Savio be appointed as the ninth (9th) member due to 
Mr. Savio’s heavy involvement with the community, specifically with regard to 
Little league.  
 
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED, that the Town Board accepts that 
recommendation and hereby appoints Jeremy Savio to the Town of Riverhead 
Recreation Advisory Committee for a two (2) year term without compensation to expire 
on December 31, 2022; and be it further  
 
RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk is hereby directed to forward a copy of this 
resolution to Jeremy Savio and the Town of Riverhead Recreation Advisory Committee; 
and be it further 
 
RESOLVED, that all Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy of 
this resolution from the electronic storage device and if needed, a certified copy of same 
may be obtained from the Office of the Town Clerk.  
 
THE VOTE 
 
RESULT: ADOPTED [UNANIMOUS] 
MOVER: Tim Hubbard, Councilman 
SECONDER: Catherine Kent, Councilwoman

Resolution 2021-27 
 
AUTHORIZES SEWER DISTRICT EMPLOYEES TO ATTEND SEMINAR 
 
Councilman Beyrodt Jr. offered the following resolution, 
which was seconded by Councilman Hubbard 
 
 WHEREAS, SUNY Morrisville is sponsoring Wastewater Treatment Operators 
Certification Courses having a specific course entitled, “Basic Lab Procedures” to be 
held on January 25, 2021 through January 29, 2021; and 
 
 WHEREAS, it is the desire of Michael Reichel, Sewer District Superintendent, 
that two (2) Sewer District employees attend such course. 
 
 NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED, that the Town Board of the Town of 
Riverhead hereby authorizes two (2) Sewer District employees to attend the 
aforementioned course to be held at SUNY Morrisville on January 25, 2021 through 
January 29, 2021; and be it further subject to the condition that in the event either newly 
hired employee leaves Town service within two years of their hire date the employee 
shall be required to reimburse the District for the cost and expense of the training 
program or at the District’s option the training cost and expense may be deducted from 
their earnings, via regular pay or SCAT (vacation, sick etc.) 
 
 RESOLVED, that all related, receipted, expenses incurred by the Sewer District 
employees, not to exceed a total cost of $1,250.00 each, shall be reimbursed upon their 
return in accordance with the Town of Riverhead Travel Policy; and be it further 
   
 RESOLVED, that all Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy of 
this resolution from the electronic storage device and, if needed, a certified copy of 
same may be obtained from the Office of the Town Clerk.

TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
 
Resolution 2021-28 
 
SETS THE FEES FOR USAGE OF RECREATION AND OTHER TOWN FACILITIES 
 
Councilman Hubbard offered the following resolution, 
which was seconded by Councilwoman Kent 
 
RESOLVED, that the Town Board does authorize the Recreation Department to 
set the following fees for the 2021 calendar year; 
 
RESOLVED, all Town Departments may review and obtain a copy of this 
resolution from the electronic storage device and if needed, a certified copy of same 
may be obtained from the Office of the Town Clerk 
 
 
 
Town of Riverhead Parks & Recreation 
2021 Facility Rental Fees  
 
$25 Application Fee Required for all Contracts 
 
Community Centers 
Single Usage Meetings     $20.00 per hour (Min. of 2 hours) 
Reoccurring Weekday Meetings during Business Hours $75.00 surcharge (every 6 
months) 
Weekend Events/Parties     $70.00 per hour (Min. of 4 
hours)** 
Non-Resident Usage Fee     $100.00 per hour (Min. of 4 
hours)** 
**Require a $100.00 Security deposit, deposit will be refunded if the building guidelines 
are followed and there is no damage to the building. 
 
Athletic Facilities 
(Ballfields/Multi-purpose/ Rink) 
Tournaments: 
Liability Insurance Required for All Tournaments 
Rental Fee-  $250 per day / field or rink   
Lights Fee-  $30 per hour / field or rink    
*required for rentals after sunset, available until 10pm. Tournament games that go past 
10pm will be charged an additional $50 per hour for lights. 
- $200.00 deposit to secure reservation. A $100.00 credit may be applied to your 
account if cancelled 1 week prior to the schedule event date.  
-Full payment is due 3 days prior to scheduled event date. 
-Full credit/refund if event is cancelled by the Town of Riverhead due to inclement 
weather/any reason. 
 
15.24
Packet Pg. 135
 
Hourly Reservations: (Leagues/Games/Practices/Camps/Clinics/General Use) 
Rental Fee- $30 per hour / field or rink  
Lights Fee- $30 per hour / field or rink  
-Fees may be credited to account if cancelled 24 hours prior to the scheduled event 
date or due to inclement weather. 
-Athletic facilities may be reserved on an hourly basis. 
-Liability Insurance required for sanctioned games, camps, clinics, leagues, and 
practices. 
 
 
Parks & Beaches 
(Group Picnics/Parties/Ceremonies) 
Park Reservations required for groups of 25+ people 
Beach reservations always required and cannot be issued during beach operation 
Parking Permits ARE required at all Town Beaches – Permits are not provided with 
Rental agreement. Parking Permits are available for purchase through the Parks & 
Recreation Department. 
 
Group Picnic/Beach Party (residents)  $50 per day + $50 attendant fee (+ $25 
application fee) 
Group Picnic/Beach Party (non-residents) $150 per day + $50 attendant fee (+ $25 
application fee) 
Gazebo/Pavilion Rental (residents)  $50 per hour + $50 attendant (+$25 
application fee) 
Gazebo/Pavilion Rental (non-residents) $100 per hour + $50 attendant (+$25 
application fee) 
 
Online Application, Rules, Regulations and Cancellation information for each facility 
may be found on our website www.riverheadrecreation.net  
 
 
Seasonal Parking Permit 
 
 Platinum Permit (Parking, 4x4, Boat Launch) 
o Resident   $100.00 
o Senior Resident   $70.00 
 
 Gold Permit (Parking, 4x4) 
o Resident   $80.00 
o Senior Resident   $65.00 
 
 Silver Permit (Parking, Boat Launch) 
o Resident   $40.00 
o Senior Resident   $10.00 
o Non-Resident   $275.00 
o Commercial   $300.00 
 
 Bronze Permit (Parking Only) 
o Resident   $20.00 
o Senior Resident   $5.00 
15.24
Packet Pg. 136
o Non-Resident   $200.00 
 
Senior Handicap Identification Permit $5.00 (no charge if valid senior resident 
parking permit) 
 
A Seasonal Parking Permit shall be required for all public beaches, parks and recreation 
facilities from January 1, 2021 to December 31, 2021. (This is an annual permit). Except 
as otherwise set forth herein; posted; set by resolution; or for purposes related to town 
sponsored recreation programs, all public beaches/parks/recreation facilities shall be 
open from 10:00am and close at 5:00pm; except for residents issued a night fishing 
permit or transient docking permit.  
 
A Beach Access Permit (4x4 Permit) shall be limited to beach access/operation of 
vehicle on Sound Beach. A Beach Access Permit shall authorize the permit holder to 
have vehicle access from January 1, 2021 to December 31, 2021 between the times: 
6:00pm to 9:00am. Note, a Fishing Club permit or Resident Night Fishing Permit is 
required for fishing between the hours of 6:00pm and 9:00am. 
 
Boat Launch Permit 
 
 Daily Boat Launch Permit      $35.00 
 
 
A Boat Launch Permit shall be required for use of boat launch at East Creek Boat 
Launch Facility. The boat launch permit shall authorize the permit holder to use the 
launch facility from April 15, 2021 to November 15, 2021. All resident, senior resident, 
and non-resident boat launch permits must be affixed to the boat and trailer. A 
commercial boat launch permit shall be affixed to the truck and trailer. A holder of a 
resident or senior resident boat launch permit may qualify for an additional 
permit/sticker provided that the second boat is owned by and registered to 
resident/senior resident permit holder. Note, in order to obtain an additional sticker, the 
permit holder must submit proof at the Recreation Office.  A holder of a commercial boat 
launch permit may qualify for more than one permit/sticker provided that the trailer is 
owned and registered to the holder of the commercial boat launch permit. Note, a 
seasonal boat launch permit is not a seasonal parking permit. All residents and 
nonresidents must possess either a seasonal parking permit, daily parking permit or 
daily boat launch permit (which shall also include a parking permit limited to use of the 
parking area at the boat launch site and valid only for date of issuance) to park vehicle 
and/or trailer at the parking area adjacent to the boat launch. A holder of a commercial 
boat launch permit may not use the parking area.  
 
 
Daily Parking Permit 
 
 Daily Permit (passenger vehicle)      $ 35.00 
   
 Daily Permit (Bus)                               $ 
85.00 
 
A daily parking permit is valid for the date issued and shall expire at the close of such 
public beach/park/recreation facility on the date of issuance.  
 
15.24
Packet Pg. 137
 
Seasonal Docking Permit (East Creek Docking Facility only) 
 
 Resident/ Taxpayer       $  85.00 per foot  
 Electricity        $  375.00 per line 
 Initial Processing Fee       $  200.00 
 Guest Parking Permit (Limit Two Per Seasonal Dock Permit)   $  
10.00 
   
A Seasonal Docking Permit shall be required for all boats/vessels seeking to use the 
docks/boat slips at East Creek Docking Facility and to use the parking area adjacent to 
East Creek Docking Facility. A seasonal docking permit shall entitle the permit holder to 
one parking permit “East Creek Docking Facility-Boat Owner Parking Permit” and, for 
additional fee, one guest parking permit “East Creek Docking Facility-Guest Parking 
Permit”. The East Creek Docking Facility season shall begin on April 15, 2021 and the 
season shall end on November 15, 2021.  All boats/vessels must be removed from the 
dock on or before the end of the season. Note, the parking permit(s) issued to the 
vessel owner, be it vessel owner or guest parking permit, are only valid for parking at 
the designated parking area adjacent to East Creek Docking Facility. A seasonal 
parking permit is required for parking at all other public beaches/parks/recreation 
facilities, including South Jamesport Beach.    
 
 
Fishing Permit 
 
 Only available to residents      $  10.00 
 
A seasonal parking permit is required to park any vehicle and/or trailer at all 
launch/beach/recreation facilities.  
 
Transient Dock Permit 
 Daily        $  25.00 
 Overnight        $  40.00 
 Weekly        $150.00 
 
*A boat/vessel may not tie-up/raft up to a boat/vessel unless specified and permitted in 
the transient dock permit. The Recreation Department reserves the right to grant or 
deny tie-up/raft up and charge an additional fee. 
  
 
 
 
Dog Park/Animal Permit 
 
 Resident        $ 10.00 
 Non-Resident        $ 25.00 
 
An owner or custodian of any animal who is issued a permit by the Recreation 
Department as set forth in Chapter 207 granting permission to enter the public bathing 
beach, recreation center, park or playground with an animal shall be required to keep 
the animal on a leash and restrained not more than six feet in length from the owner or 
custodian at all times and shall not permit the animal to defecate or urinate in the vicinity 
of other persons. If an animal defecates in any public bathing beach, recreation center 
15.24
Packet Pg. 138
or facility, park, or playground, the owner shall immediately collect the feces and 
remove it from the public bathing beach, recreation center or facility, park, or 
playground. Notwithstanding the above, no permit for dogs/animals shall authorize use 
of a public bathing beach during hours set forth for public bathing and swimming. 
 
All permits issued by the Town of Riverhead may not be duplicated, photocopied or 
transferred. If a permit is lost or stolen, please contact the Town of Riverhead 
Recreation Department.

Packet Pg. 142
INVESTMENT POLICY  
FOR THE  
TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
 
I SCOPE 
 
This investment policy applies to all moneys and other financial resources available for 
investment on its behalf or on behalf of any other entity or individual. 
 
II OBJECTIVES 
 
The primary objectives of the local government’s investment activities are, in primary 
order: 
 
- to conform with all applicable federal, state and other legal requirements 
- to adequately safeguard principal  
- to provide sufficient liquidity to meet all operating requirements; and 
- to obtain a reasonable rate of return  
 
III DELEGATION OF AUTHORITY 
 
The Town Board’s responsibility for administration of the investment program is delegated 
to the Town Supervisor who shall establish written procedures for the operation of the  
investment program consistent with these investment guidelines.  Such procedures shall include 
an adequate internal control structure to provide a satisfactory level of accountability based on 
a database or records incorporating description and amounts of investments, transaction dates 
and other relevant information and regulate the activities of subordinate employees. 
 
IV PRUDENCE 
 
All participants in the investment process shall seek to act responsibly as custodians of 
the public trust and shall avoid any transaction that might impair public confidence in the Town 
of Riverhead to govern effectively. 
 
Investments shall be made with judgement and care, under circumstances then 
prevailing, which persons of prudence, discretion and intelligence exercise in the management 
of their own affairs, not for speculation, but for investment, considering the safety of the 
principal as well as the probable income to be derived. 
   
All participants involved in the investment process shall refrain from personal business 
activity that could conflict with proper execution of the investment program, or which could 
impair their ability to make impartial investment decisions. 
 
V DIVERSIFICATION 
 
It is the policy of the Town of Riverhead to diversify its deposits and investment s by 
financial institution, by investment instrument, and by maturity scheduling. 
 
 
15.25.a
Packet Pg. 143
Attachment: Investment Policy 2020 [Revision 1]  (2021-29 : Adopts Investment Policy)
 
VI INTERNAL CONTROLS 
 
It is the policy of the Town of Riverhead for all moneys collected by any officer or 
employee of the government to transfer those funds to the Town Supervisor within ten days of 
deposit, or within the time period specified in law, whichever is shorter. 
 
The Town Supervisor as chief fiscal officer is responsible for establishing and maintaining 
an internal control structure to provide reasonable, but no t absolute, assurance that deposits 
and investments are safeguarded against loss from unauthorized use or disposition, that 
transactions are executed in accordance with management’s authorization and recorded 
properly, and are managed in compliance with applicable laws and regulations. 
 
The Town Board, in order to implement the investment policy, authori zes the Financial 
Administrator or his deputy, under the control and direction of the Financial Administrator, to 
undertake the necessary ministerial day to  day functions regarding the temporary investments  
of monies and to further carry out the cash management for town funds and related collateral 
management matters subject to and consistent with General Municipal Law, Section 11.  
 
VII DESIGNATION OF DEPOSITORIES 
 
  The banks and trust companies authorized for the deposit of Town funds: 
   
 Depository Name  
 
Peoples United Bank, BNB, Capital One , Citibank, Chase, Bank United, First 
National Bank of Lo ng Island, Sterling National Bank , TD Bank , Flushing Bank, 
Hanover Bank. 
 
VIII COLLATERALIZATION OF DEPOSITS 
 
In accordance with the provisions of General Municipal Law, Section 10, all deposits of the Town 
of Riverhead, including certificates of deposits and special time deposits, in excess of the amount 
insured under the provisions of the Federal Deposit Insurance Act shall be secured pursuant to 
and consistent with the provisions of General Municipal Law, Section 10 (3) (a) through (d): 
 
1) By a pledge of “eligible securities” with an aggregate “market value”, or provided 
by General Municipal Law, Section 10, equal to the 102% of the aggregate amount 
of deposits from the categories designated Appendix A to this policy.  
 
2) By an eligible “irrevocable letter of credit” issued by a qualified bank other than 
the bank with the deposits in favor of the government for a term not to exceed 90 
days with an aggregate value equal to 140% of the aggregate amount of deposits 
and the agreed upon interest, if any.  A qualified bank is one whose commercial 
paper and other unsecured short-term debt obligations are rated in one of the 
three highest rating categories by at least one nationally recognized statistical 
rating organization or by a bank that is in compliance with applicable federal 
minimum risk-based capital requirements. 
15.25.a
Packet Pg. 144
Attachment: Investment Policy 2020 [Revision 1]  (2021-29 : Adopts Investment Policy)
3) By an eligible “irrevocable letter of credit” issued in favor of the local government 
by a federal home loan bank whose commercial paper and other unsecured short-
term debt obligations are rated in the highest rating category by at least one 
nationally recognized statistical rating organization, for the payment of 100% of 
the aggregate amount of public deposits from such officers and the agreed upon 
interest, if any. 
 
4) By an eligible surety bond payable to the government for an amount at least equal 
to 100% of the aggregate amount of deposits and the agreed upon interest, if 
any, executed by an insurance company authorized to do business in New York 
State, whose claims – paying ability is rated in the highest rating category by at 
least two nationally recognized statistical rating organizations.  The terms and 
conditions of any eligible surety bond shall be approved by the governing board. 
5) Under the amendment of General Municipal Law Chapter 128, Sections 10 and 11, 
the Town may authorize their designated depository bank or trust company to 
arrange for the “redeposit” of the local government’s funds, for the account of the 
local government, in one or more “banking institut ion,” through a deposit 
placement program, subject to a placement program that meets criteria set forth 
in Section 10 (2)(a)(ii)(A) through (D).  
 
IX SAFEKEEPING AND COLLATERALIZATION 
 
Eligible securities used for collateralizing deposits shall be held by the depository and/or 
a third party bank or trust company subject to security and custodial agreements. The Supervisor 
shall be authorized to execute all and any necessary security, custodian and collateral 
agreements on behalf of the Town of Riverhead. 
 
The security agreement shall provide that eligible securities are being pledged to secure 
the Town’s deposits together with agreed upon interest, if any, and any costs or expenses arising 
out of the collections of such deposits upon default.  It shall also provide the conditions under 
which the securities may be sold, presented for payment, substituted or released and the events 
which will enable the Town of Riverhead to exercise it rights against the pledged securities.  In 
the event that the securities are  not registered or i nscribed in the name of the Town of 
Riverhead, such securities shall be delivered in a form suitable for transfer or with an assignment 
in blank to the Town of Riverhead or its custodial bank. 
 
The custodial agreement shall provide that securities held by the bank or trust company, 
or agent of and custodian for the Town of Riverhead, will be kept separate and apart from the 
general assets of the custodian bank or trust company and will not, in any circumstances, be 
commingled with or become part of the backing for any other deposit or other liabilities.  The 
agreement should also describe that the custodian shall confirm the receipt, substitution or 
release of the securities.  The agreement shall provide from the frequency of revaluation of 
eligible securities and for the substitute of securities when a change in the rating of a security 
may cause in-eligibility. 
 
 
 
 
15.25.a
Packet Pg. 145
Attachment: Investment Policy 2020 [Revision 1]  (2021-29 : Adopts Investment Policy)
X PERMITTED INVESTMENTS 
 
As authorized by General Municipal Law, Section 11, the Town Board authorizes the chief 
fiscal officer to invest moneys not required for immediate expenditure in the following types of 
investment: 
  
- Special time deposit accounts; 
- Certificates of deposit; 
- Obligations of the United States of America; 
- Obligations guaranteed by agencies of the United States of America where the 
payment of principal and interest are guaranteed by the United States of 
America;  
- Obligations of the State of New York; 
- Obligations issued pursuant to Local Finance Law Section 24.00 or 25.00 (with 
approval of the State Comptroller) by any municipality, school district or district 
cooperation other than the Town of Riverhead; 
- Obligations of public authorities, public housing authorities, urban renewal 
agencies and industrial development agencies where the general State statutes 
governing such entities or whose specific enabling legislation authorizes such 
investments; 
- Certificates of Participation (COPs) issued pursuant to GML, Section 109-b; 
- Obligations of the Town of Riverhead, b ut only with any moneys in a reserve 
fund established pursuant to GML, Section 6-c, 6-d, 6-e, 6-g, 6-h, 6-j, 6-k, 6-l, 
6-m, or 6-n. 
 
All investment obligations shall be payable or redeemable at the option of the Town of 
Riverhead within such times as the p roceeds will be needed to meet expenditures for 
purposes for which the moneys were provided and, in the case of obligations purchased 
with the proceeds of bonds or notes, shall be payable or redeemable at the option of the 
Town of Riverhead within two years of the date of purchase. 
 
XI AUTHORIZED FINANACIAL INSTITUTES AND DEALERS 
 
The Town of Riverhead shall maintain a list of financial institutions and dealers 
approved for investment purposes and establish appropriate limits to the amount of 
investments which can be made with each financial institution or dealer.  All financial 
institutions with which the Town conducts business must be credit worthy.  Banks shall 
provide their most recent Consolidated Report of Condition at the request of the Town.  
Security dealers not affiliated with a bank shall be required to be classified as reporting 
dealers affiliated with the New York Federal Reserve Bank, as primary dealers.  The chief 
fiscal officer is responsible for evaluating the financial position and maintaini ng a listing 
of proposed depositories, trading partners and custodians. Such listing shall be evaluated 
at least annually. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
15.25.a
Packet Pg. 146
Attachment: Investment Policy 2020 [Revision 1]  (2021-29 : Adopts Investment Policy)
XII PURCHASE OF INVESTMENT 
 
The chief fiscal officer is authorized to contract for the purposes of investment: 
 
1) By participating in a cooperative investment program with another 
authorized governmental entity pursuant to Article 5G of the General 
Municipal Law where such program meets all the requirements set 
forth in the Office of the State Comptroller Opinion #88-46, and the 
specific program has been authorized by the Town Board. 
 
2) By utilizing an ongoing investment program with an authorized 
tracking partner pursuant to a contract authorized by the Town 
Board. 
 
All purchased obligations, unless registered or inscribed in the name of the Town 
of Riverhead, shall be purchased through, delivered to and held in the custody of a bank 
or trust company.  Such obligations shall be purchased, sold or presented for redemption 
or payment by such bank in trust company only in acco rdance with prior written 
authorization from the officer authorized to make the investment.  All such transactions 
shall be confirmed in writing to the Town of Riverhead by the bank or trust company.  
Any obligation held in the custody of a bank or trust c ompany shall be held pursuant to 
a written custodial agreement as described in General Municipal Law, Section 10. 
 
 The custodial agreement shall provide that securities held by the bank or trust 
company, as agent of and custodian for, the Town of Riverhea d, will be kept separate 
and apart from the general assets of the custodian bank or trust company and will not, 
in any circumstances, be commingled with or become part of the backing for any other 
deposit or other liabilities.  The agreement shall describe how the custodian shall confirm 
the receipt and release of the securities.  Such agreement shall include all provisions 
necessary to provide the Town a perfected interest in the securities.

Appendix A 
 
Schedule of Eligible Securities 
 
 
_____ (i)  Obligations issued, or fully insured or guaranteed as to the payment of principal and 
interest, by the United states of America, an agency thereof or a United States 
government sponsored corporation. 
 
_____ (ii)  Obligations issued or fully guaranteed by the International Bank for Reconstruction 
and Development, the Inter-American Development Bank, the Asian Development Bank, 
and the African Development Bank. 
 
_____ (iii)  Obligations partially insured or guaranteed by any agency of the United States of 
America, at a proportion of the Market Value of the obligation that represents the 
amount of the insurance or guaranty. 
 
_____ (iv)  Obligations issued or fully insured or guaranteed by the State of New York, 
obligations issued by a municipal corporation, school district or district corporation of 
such State or obligations of any public benefit corporation which under a specific State 
statute may be accepted as security for deposit of public moneys. 
 
_____ (v)  Obligations issued by states (other than the State of New York) of the United States 
rated in one of the three highest rating categories by at least one nationally recognized 
statistical organization. 
 
_____ (vi)  Obligations of Puerto Rico rated in one of the three highest rating categories by at 
least one nationally recognized statistical rating organization. 
 
_____ (vii)  Obligations of counties, cities and other governmental entities of a state other 
than the State of New York having the power to levy taxes that are backed by the full 
faith and credit of such governmental entity and rated in one of the three highest rating 
categories by at least one nationally recognized statistical rating organization.  
 
_____ (viii)  Obligations of domestic corporations rated in one of the two highest rating 
categories by at least one nationally recognized statistical rating organization. 
 
_____  (ix)  Any mortgage related securities, as defined in the Securities Exchange Act of 
1934, as amended, which may be purchased by banks under the limitations established 
by bank regulatory agencies. 
 
_____ (x)  Commercial paper and bankers acceptances issued by a bank other than the Bank, 
rated in the highest short term category by at least one nationally recognized statistical 
rating organization and having maturities of not longer than 60 days from the date they 
are pledged. 
 
_____  (xi)  Zero obligations of the United States government related as "Treasury strips".

Resolution 2021-31 
 
MAINTAINS POLICY OF NON-DISCRIMINATION 
 
Councilman Hubbard offered the following resolution, 
which was seconded by Councilwoman Kent 
 
WHEREAS, pursuant to United States Code Title 42, Section 6101 et seq., 
Section 200d, and Title 29, Section 794, it is required that a formal policy of 
nondiscrimination be established for the Town of Riverhead. 
 
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED, that the following policy of 
nondiscrimination be, and hereby is, established: 
 
No person shall, on the grounds of race, color, national origin, sex, 
religion, age, disability status, veteran status, familial status, sexual orientation,  
genetic information, or any other status protected under Federal statute be 
excluded from participation in or be subjected to discrimination in any program 
or activity funded, in whole or part, by Federal funds. 
 
 The Town of Riverhead has designated the following person as the 
contact to coordinate efforts to comply with this requirement: 
 
Yvette Aguiar 
Town Supervisor, Town of Riverhead 
200 Howell Avenue 
Riverhead, NY  11901-2596 
Telephone:  631-727-3200 
Monday –Friday: 8:30 A.M. to 4:30 P.M 
 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk be, and hereby is, directed to 
publish a copy of the above policy of nondiscrimination in the January 14, 2021 issue of 
the official newspaper and to post same on the signboard in Town Hall.

TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
 
Resolution 2021-33 
 
SCAVENGER WASTE DISTRICT BUDGET ADJUSTMENT 
 
Councilman Beyrodt Jr. offered the following resolution, 
which was seconded by Councilman Hubbard 
 
WHEREAS, the Scavenger Waste District has implemented a new system for 
collecting tipping fees, and 
 
WHEREAS, due to this new system, additional revenues have been collected 
above the amount budgeted for in the 2020 budget and, 
 
WHEREAS, the Scavenger Waste District has also incurred bank fees and larger 
electricity expenses than anticipated during 2020.  Now, therefore be it 
 
RESOLVED, that the Supervisor be, and is hereby authorized to establish the 
following budget adjustments; 
 
 FROM  TO 
 
ES5-2122-000-00000-8 – Sewer Charges  $76,000.00 
 
 ES5-8-8189-472-000-00000 – Utilities Electricity   $45,000.00 
 ES5-8-8189-490-000-00000 – Misc Bank Fees    $31,000.00 
 
And be it further 
 
RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk is hereby authorized to forward a copy of this 
resolution to the Accounting and Sewer Departments, and be it further 
 
RESOLVED, all Town Hall Departments may review and obtain a copy of this 
resolution from the electronic storage device and if needed, a certified copy of same 
may be obtained from the Office of the Town Clerk. 
 
THE VOTE 
 
RESULT: ADOPTED [UNANIMOUS] 
MOVER: Frank Beyrodt Jr., Councilman 
SECONDER: Tim Hubbard, Councilman 
AYES: Yvette Aguiar, Tim Hubbard, Catherine Kent, Frank Beyrodt Jr.

Resolution 2021-37 
 
RELEASES PERFORMANCE SECURITY OF RIVERHEAD SOUND ASSOCIATES 
LLC AND ACCEPTS ONE-YEAR MAINTENANCE SECURITY IN CONNECTION 
WITH THE SUBDIVISION ENTITLED "AQUEBOGUE GOLF RESORTS" 
 
Councilman Hubbard offered the following resolution, 
which was seconded by Councilwoman Kent 
 
WHEREAS, the final plat of a certain subdivision map was approved by resolution 
adopted on June 4, 2003 by the Riverhead Planning Board, which resolution granted 
conditional approval to the final plat entitled, "Aquebogue Golf Resorts”, filed by Riverhead 
Sound Associates LLC; and  
 
WHEREAS, plans for the construction of various improvements to roads known 
and designated as STONECROP ROAD, WAKE ROBIN LANE, WOLFSBANE COURT 
and FOXGLOVE ROW, for ponds and other drainage improvements were submitted to 
the Planning Board of the Town of Riverhead; and 
  
WHEREAS, the roads known and designated as STONECROP ROAD, WAKE 
ROBIN LANE, WOLFSBANE COURT and FOXGLOVE ROW, the ponds and other 
related drainage improvements are not intended to be dedicated to the Town of 
Riverhead and instead will remain in private ownership/control; and 
WHEREAS, pursuant to Riverhead Town Board Resolution #30 adopted on January 
6, 2004, the Town Board accepted International Fidelity Insurance Company Performance 
Bond #0371109 in the amount of One Million Nine Hundred Ten Thousand 00/100 
($1,910,000.00) Dollars; and 
WHEREAS, pursuant to Riverhead Town Board Resolution #113, adopted on February 
6, 2008, the Town Board authorized a reduction of the above referenced performance 
security to the amount of $636,666.67; and 
 WHEREAS, pursuant to further Riverhead Town Board resolutions, the Town Board 
has granted additional 2-year extensions of aforesaid performance security; and 
 
WHEREAS, said roads, drainage systems, sumps and other improvements have 
been completed in accordance with the plans and specifications of the Town of 
Riverhead Planning Board; and  
 
WHEREAS, in accordance with Subdivision Inspection Memo from Vincent A. 
Gaudiello, P.E., dated July 15, 2020, it has been determined that the construction of the 
said roads, drainage systems, sumps and other improvements within the subdivision 
have been substantially completed.  
 
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED, in accordance with the above, the Town 
Board of the Town of Riverhead, hereby adopts the recommendations made by Vincent 
A. Gaudiello, P.E. and determines that said roads, drainage systems, sumps and other 
15.33
Packet Pg. 165
improvements have been completed in accordance with the plans and specifications of 
the Town of Riverhead Planning Board; and be it further  
 
RESOLVED, that upon the Town Board’s receipt of one-year maintenance 
security in the amount of $636,666.67, which will shall be in a form to be reviewed and 
approved by the Town Attorney and be for a term of October 31, 2020 to October 31, 
2021, International Fidelity Insurance Company Performance Bond #0371109 and any 
and all previously submitted performance bonds/security shall be deemed discharged 
and released, and the Town Clerk shall return the original of said Performance Bond 
#0371109 to International Fidelity Insurance Company, Attn: George Rettig, Asst. Vice 
President, Claims Counsel, One Newark Center, 20th Floor, Newark, New Jersey 
07102; and be it further 
 
RESOLVED, , that the Town Clerk is hereby directed to forward a copy of this

TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
NOTICE TO BIDDERS 
Sealed bids for ANNUAL HVAC SERVICE CONTRACT, for  use by the Town of 
Riverhead, will be received by the Town Clerk of the Town of Riverhead at Town Hall, 
200 Howell Avenue, Riverhead, New York, 11901, until 11:00 a.m. on January 28, 2021, 
at which time all bids received shall be opened and read aloud. 
Bid Specifications and/or Plans may be obtained by visiting the Town of Riverhead 
website at www.townofriverheadny.gov on or after January 14, 2021 .  Click on “Bid 
Requests” and follow the instructions to register.   
All bids are to be submitted in a sealed envelope bearing the designation ANNUAL HVAC 
SERVICE CONTRACT.  All bids must be submitted on the bid form provided.  Any and 
all exceptions to the S pecifications must be listed on a separate sheet of paper, 
bearing the designation "EXCEPTIONS TO THE SPECIFICATIONS" and be attached to 
the bid form. 
NOTE: Bid responses must be delivered to  the Office of the Town Clerk at 200 
Howell Avenue, Riverhead, New York, 11901, on or before January 28, 2021 at 11:00 
a.m.  The Town may declin e to accept, deem untimely and/or reject any bid 
response/proposal that is not delivered to the Office of the Town Clerk. 
The Town Board reserves the right and responsibility to reject any or all bids or to waive 
any formality if it believes such action to be in the best interest of the Town. 
BY ORDER OF THE TOWN BOARD 
O F  T H E  T O W N  O F  R I V E R H E A D  
D I A N E  M .  W I L H E L M ,  T O W N  C L E R K

Councilman Beyrodt Jr. offered the following resolution,

2020, was authorized to publish and post a Notice to Bidders for proposals for the

December 4, 2020 
 
 
MEMO TO:  Teresa Baldinucci, 
   Purchasing Agent 
 
FROM:  Shannon Clifford 
   Principal Office Assistant, Engineering Department 
 
RE:   Street Light & Traffic Signal Maintenance Repair Parts Bid 
 
Engineering has reviewed the above referenced Bid Submissions and would like 
the Bid to be awarded as per the attached documentation. Please note a list of 
substitution determinations below. Please also find attached our documentation for 
such determinations. Please let us know if you have any questions.  
 
Bid Item #MC1– Award to Schwing Electrical. Their substitution is for LED and 
therefore is acceptable whereas, the substitution for Revco is for HID, incandescent 
only. 
 
Bid Item # MC2, MC5, MC6-MC10, BC6 – Award to Schwing Electrical - 
Substitution is equal or greater to required specification. 
 
BID Item #ME1 – Award to Revco - Schwing price based on plastic cover not the 
required steel cover.  
 
BID Item #ME10 – Award to Revco – They have confirmed their price is based on 
a roll of 100ft making price $0.63 per foot.

and Emergency Services for the Riverhead Water District and is hereby awarded to:

January 1, 2021 through December 31, 2021

Supervisor be and is hereby authorized to execute a contract with Philip Ross 
Industries, Inc., and

to release to the successful bidder the bidder’s bid security, and be it further

On Thursday, December 03, 2020, bids were received by the Town of Riverhead on behalf of the 
Riverhead Water District for the above referenced project.  A total of three (3) bids for the above contract 
were submitted, with the lowest being submitted by:   
 
Philip Ross Industries, Inc.  
88 Duryea Road 
Melvile, NY 11747

the low bidder as  $93,192.00 for January 1, 2022 thru December 31, 2022, and $99,608.00 for January 
1, 2023 thru December 31, 2023.

similar maintenance contracts for other Districts. We recommend the District award the contract for the 
period of  January 1, 2 021 thru December 31, 2021 to Philip Ross Industries Inc. in the amount of 
$86,970.00.

H2M architects + engineers

EMERGENCY SERVICES RIVERHEAD WATER DISTRICT  PROJECT NO. 
RDWD2003 (WD) 
 
Councilwoman Kent offered the following resolution, 
which was seconded by Councilman Beyrodt Jr. 
 
WHEREAS, this Town Board did authorize the advertisement for bids for Water 
Distribution System Maintenance and Emergency Services for the Riverhead Water 
District, and  
 
WHEREAS, all bids received were opened and read aloud o n the date and time 
advertised in the notice, and  
 
WHEREAS, H2M Group, consulting engineers to the Riverhead Water District, 
by attached letter dated December 16, 2020 did recommend that the bid be awarded to 
Excav Services Inc. for the total bid amount o f $400,794.69 for the period of January 1, 
2021 through December 31, 2021. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED , that the bid for Water Distribution 
System Maintenance and Emergency Services for the Riverhead Water District and is 
hereby awarded to:  
 
Excav Services Inc. 
Manorville, New York 
In the total bid amount of $400,794.69 for period 
January 1, 2021 through December 31, 2021 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED , that Excav Services Inc. shall post a bond in the 
amount of the total bid of $400,794.69, and  
 
BE IT FURTH ER RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk forward a certified copy of 
this resolution to the above-named contractor, and  
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk is hereby authorized to return 
to all unsuccessful bidders their respective bid security, and  
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that pursuant to this bid award, the Town 
Supervisor be and is hereby authorized to execute a contract with Excav Services Inc., 
and  
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that upon completion of fully executed contracts 
and the filing of said con tract with the Town Clerk, the Town Clerk is hereby authorized 
to release to the successful bidder the bidder’s bid security, and be it further

01.05.2021 ADOPTED 
 
 
TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
 
Resolution 2021-45 
 
AWARDS BID – HYDRAULIC CONTROL VALVE MAINTENANCE AND 
EMERGENCY SERVICES RIVERHEAD WATER DISTRICT  PROJECT NO. 
RDWD2003 (HCV) 
 
Councilman Beyrodt Jr. offered the following resolution, 
which was seconded by Councilman Hubbard 
 
WHEREAS, this Town Board did authorize the advertisement for bids for 
Hydraulic Control Valve Maintenance and Emergency Services for the Riverhead Water 
District, and  
 
WHEREAS, all bids received were opened and read aloud on the date and time 
advertised in the notice, and  
 
WHEREAS, H2M Group, consulting engineers to the Riverhead Water District, 
by attached letter dated December 16, 2020 did recommend that the bid be awarded to 
Harper Haines Fluid Control, Inc. for the total bid amount of $74,720.96 for the period of 
January 1, 2021 through December 31, 2021. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that the bid for Hydraulic Control Valve 
Maintenance and Emergency Services for the Riverhead Water District and is hereby 
awarded to:  
 
Harper Haines Fluid Control, Inc. 
Milford, Connecticut 
In the total bid amount of $74,720.96 for period 
January 1, 2021 through December 31, 2021 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that Harper Haines Fluid Control, Inc. shall post a 
bond in the amount of the total bid of $74,270.96, and  
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk forward a certified copy of 
this resolution to the above-named contractor, and  
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk is hereby authorized to return 
to all unsuccessful bidders their respective bid security, and  
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that pursuant to this bid award, the Town 
Supervisor be and is hereby authorized to execute a contract with Harper Haines Fluid 
Control, Inc., and  
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that upon completion of fu lly executed contracts 
and the filing of said contract with the Town Clerk, the Town Clerk is hereby authorized 
to release to the successful bidder the bidder’s bid security, and be it further

Board Members: 
On Thursday, December 03, 2020, bids were received by the Town of Riverhead on behalf of the 
Riverhead Water District for the above referenced project.  A total  of one (1) bids for the above contract 
were submitted, with the lowest being submitted by:   
Harper Haines Fluid Control, Inc. 
125 Old Gate Lane 
Milford, CT 06460  
The Bid Comparison Total as submitted by the low bidder is $74,270.96 for the period of January 1, 2021 
thru December 31, 2021. Optional one- and two-year extensions were included with this bid, submitted by 
the low bidder as  $58,094.54 for January 1, 2022 thru December 31, 2022, and $63,676.28 for January 
1, 2023 thru December 31, 2023. 
We have investigated the qualifications submitted by the bidder and have found them to be a qualified and 
responsible contractor.  Harper Haines Fluid Control, Inc . has su ccessfully completed various similar 
projects over the past few years.  It is our opinion that Harper Haines Fluid Control, Inc . is qualified, and 
their bid prices are fair and reasonable. We recommend the District award the contract for the period of  
January 1, 2021 thru December 31, 2021 to Harper Haines Fluid Control, Inc. in the amount of $74,270.96. 
Should you have any questions regarding this recommendation, please feel free to contact our office.  
Very truly yours, 
H2M architects + engineers

01.05.2021 ADOPTED 
 
 
TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
 
Resolution 2021-47 
 
AWARD AND REJECT BIDS FOR CHEMICALS FOR RIVERHEAD SEWER 
DISTRICT 
 
Councilwoman Kent offered the following resolution, 
which was seconded by Councilman Beyrodt Jr. 
 
WHEREAS, the Town Board resolution #2020-537 authorized the Town Clerk to 
publish and post a notice for sealed bids for CHEMICALS FOR RIVERHEAD SEWER 
DISTRICT and; 
 
WHEREAS, 9 bids were received, opened and read aloud in the Office of the 
Town Clerk, 200 Howell Avenue, Riverhead, NY 11901 on November 12, 2020 at 
11:00 AM; and 
 
WHEREAS, the bids were reviewed and a bid tabulation was prepared by the 
Purchasing Agent and presented to the Town Board for review; and 
 
WHEREAS, the low bidder for Item #7 and Item #9, Brenntag Northeast LLC, 
has subsequently notified the Purchasing Agent that they are rescinding their bid due to 
inability to supply product; and 
  
WHEREAS, the Sewer District Superintendent has reviewed the bids submitted 
and has submitted his recommendations to the Purchasing Agent, including rejecting all 
bids for Item #8 and going to the next lowest bidder for Item #7 and Item #9. 
 
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED that the bid for CHEMICALS FOR 
RIVERHEAD SEWER DISTRICT be awarded to the vendors indicated below for items 
indicated at prices on the attached page; 
 
Items #1, 4, 6, 7 & 9 to  George S. Coyne Chemical Co., Inc. 
3015 State Rd 
Croydon, PA 19021 
bidadministration@coynechemical.com 
  
 
Item #2 to    Kemira Water Solutions, Inc. 
4321 W 6th St 
Lawrence, KS 66049 
Kwsna.bids@kemira.com 
 
Item #3 to    Progressive Growers 
1129 Cross River Dr 
Riverhead, NY 11901 
mmatulonis@progressivegrower.com 
15.43
Packet Pg. 225
 
Item #5 to   Shannon Chemical Corp. 
PO Box 376 
Malverne, PA 19355 
dcflynn@shannonchem.com 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Town Board rejects all bids for Item #8; 
and  
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Town Clerk be and is hereby authorized 
to forward a copy of this resolution to all vendors listed above, the Purchasing 
Department and the Sewer District; and 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that all Town Hall Departments may review and 
obtain a copy of this resolution from the electronic storage device and, if needed, a 
certified copy of same may be obtained from the Office of the Town Clerk. 
 
THE VOTE 
 
RESULT: ADOPTED [UNANIMOUS] 
MOVER: Catherine Kent, Councilwoman 
SECONDER: Frank Beyrodt Jr., Councilman 
AYES: Yvette Aguiar, Tim Hubbard, Catherine Kent, Frank Beyrodt Jr.

01.05.2021 ADOPTED 
 
 
TOWN OF RIVERHEAD 
 
Resolution 2021-49 
 
NUNC PRO TUNC EXTENSION OF BID FOR DOWFLAKE EXTRA 83-87% CALCIUM 
CHLORIDE OR EQUAL 
 
Councilman Hubbard offered the following resolution, 
which was seconded by Councilwoman Kent 
 
WHEREAS, pursuant to Resolution #2019-875 adopted by the Town Board on 
December 3, 2019, the Town of Riverhead extended the bid for Dowflake Xtra 83-87% 
Calcium Chloride or Equal to Long Island Cauliflower Association for the second of up 
to 3 additional years; and 
 
 WHEREAS, the Buildings & Grounds Department has requested that the 
contract with Long Island Cauliflower Association be extended nunc pro tunc for the 
third and last additional year beginning November 21, 2020 pursuant to the terms of the 
current contract; and 
  
 WHEREAS, Long Island Cauliflower Association has agreed to extend the 
contract until November 20, 2021 per the attached Bid Extension Request for the 
original bid amounts under the same terms and conditions set forth in original contract 
as attached hereto and constitutes the second extension pursuant to the terms of the 
current bid contract; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Town Board has reviewed said request. 
  
 NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED, the Town Board approves the extension 
nunc pro tunc of the contract for Dowflake Xtra 83-87% Calcium Chloride or Equal with 
Long Island Cauliflower Association for one (1) year beginning November 21, 2020 for 
the original bid amounts under the same terms and conditions set forth in original 
contract; and  
 
 BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that all Town Hall Departments may review and 
obtain a copy of this resolution from the electronic storage device and, if needed, a copy 
of same may be obtained from the office of the Town Clerk. 
 
THE VOTE 
 
RESULT: ADOPTED [UNANIMOUS] 
MOVER: Tim Hubbard, Councilman 
SECONDER: Catherine Kent, Councilwoman 
AYES: Yvette Aguiar, Tim Hubbard, Catherine Kent, Frank Beyrodt Jr. 
 
15.45

Resolution 2021-51 
 
ADOPTS SPECIAL EVENT APPLICATION FOR NY MARINE RESCUE CENTER 5K 
RUN FOR THE RIDLEY (SATURDAY MAY 1, 2021) 
 
Councilman Beyrodt Jr. offered the following resolution, 
which was seconded by Councilman Hubbard 
 
WHEREAS, on March 31, 2020, NY Marine Rescue Center, submitted a Chapter 
255 Application for the purpose of conducting their 23rd Annual 5K Run for the Ridley 
along  Main Street, Riverhead, New York, on Saturday May 1, 2021, between the hours 
of 5 am to 1 pm and 
 
WHEREAS, EEAC has completed and filed a Short Environmental Assessment 
Form in accordance with 6 NYCRR 617; and 
 
WHEREAS, the applicant has paid the requisite Chapter 255 Application fee of 
$150.00; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Town Clerk’s office of the Town of Riverhead has reviewed all 
documents regarding said application. 
 
WHEREAS, A Comprehensive Liability Insurance Policy naming the Town of 
Riverhead, as an additional insured in the amount of $1,000,000 per occurrence                    
and $2,000,000 general aggregate.  Liquor Liability Insurance naming the Town of 
Riverhead as an additional insured in the amount of $2,000,000 per occurrence and 
$2,000,000 general aggregate has been received; and 
 
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED, that the Town Board of the Town of 
Riverhead hereby has classified the action as a Type II under SEQRA NYCRR 617.5 C 
(21) and requiring no further environmental review; and be it further 
 
RESOLVED, that the Chapter 255 Application for the NY Marine Rescue Center 
for the purpose of conducting their 23rd Annual 5K Run for the Ridley along Main Street, 
Riverhead, New York, on the aforesaid date and time, is hereby approved; and be it 
further

necessary tent permits, and any all electric shall comply with the applicable provisions 
of the Building and Fire Code of New York State, the National Electrical Code and 
National Fire Protection Association 102 (Tents & Membrane Structures); and be it 
further 
 
RESOLVED, that this approval is subject to the provisions of Riverhead Town 
Code Chapter 301-251 G (7) entitled “Signs” and any other section of the Riverhead 
Town Code that may pertain to this event; and be it further
